People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I guess what really made me come in is... things at work are just not right. I'm a wholesale buyer, and lately, I just can't shake the feeling that people at work—and even some of our vendors—are tracking my buys. Like, they're secretly messing with the launch numbers just to make me look stupid. My supervisor keeps pulling up the reports to show me they don't prove that, but I know what's happening. 

And honestly, the vendors are something else. There's this one guy I deal with, he always wears these ridiculously bright polo shirts. Neon orange, mostly. It reminds me of those traffic cones they used to put up near my old high school. They were doing all this construction on the main road for, like, three years straight when I was a teenager. I used to have to take this crazy detour through a neighborhood where this one guy had these little ceramic gnomes in his yard. Hundreds of them, just staring at the street. It was super weird, my buddy and I used to count them. But yeah... anyway, I just know they're altering my numbers.

I mean, no, not really. People usually just nod and agree with me, so I assume they're following along just fine. Like, just yesterday I was talking to the guy at the deli counter about the new vendor shipments we're dealing with at work, and he seemed totally on board. Speaking of the deli, they have this really good spicy mustard there now. It's got those little seeds in it? Reminds me of the mustard my uncle used to put on his hot dogs at the Fourth of July cookouts. He'd always burn the hot dogs, like, pitch black on the outside. We had to scrape the char off with a plastic knife. One time, my cousin accidentally swallowed a piece of the plastic knife because it snapped off in the meat. We had to take him to the emergency room, and we missed the fireworks. They were doing the big ones over the lake that year, too. You could hear the booms echoing off the water from miles away. It sounded like thunder. I love thunderstorms, honestly, the way the sky turns that weird purple color right before it pours. 

But yeah, no, I don't think people have trouble following me. They get it.

Squeak, squeak, squeak. Drove me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think I confuse people when I talk. I make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have trouble following your train of thought when you're explaining something?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. Speaking of the deli, they have this really good spicy mustard there now. It's got those little seeds in it? She was always so particular about her clothes. She used to iron everything, even my jeans when I was in high school. I hated it. They always felt so stiff, you know? Like cardboard. I used to try and break them in by doing squats in my bedroom before the bus came. But yeah, the numbers... they're definitely altering them. I'm not crazy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense when you're talking about what's going on at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think they make perfect sense. I know what I'm talking about when I say they're messing with my numbers. It's clear as day to me. But sometimes... I don't know, maybe I do get a little sidetracked. Like right now, I just remembered this one time I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the vendors, and I got totally distracted by this stain on his tie. It looked exactly like the shape of Florida. I went to Florida once when I was a kid. We drove all the way down to Orlando, and the AC in my dad's car broke halfway there. It was miserable. We had to roll all the windows down, and the wind was so loud we couldn't even hear the radio. My dad was playing this old cassette tape of the Eagles on repeat. I still can't listen to \"Hotel California\" without sweating. But anyway, yeah, I think my thoughts are connected. I just... I notice things, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay focused on one topic when talking about something important?\n\nPatient: I don't know, I haven't really noticed that being a problem. I feel like I stay pretty focused on the important stuff. Like, when I'm looking at the purchase orders, I'm locked in. Though, they just switched the supplier for our printer paper at the office, and it's so thin now. It feels exactly like the tissue paper my grandma used to stuff inside my birthday cards. She always put a crisp five-dollar bill in there. Never ten, always five. I used to take that five bucks down to the corner store and buy those little wax bottles with the colored syrup inside. You know the ones? You bite the top off... they tasted like pure plastic, honestly, but my buddy and I loved them. The guy who ran the store had this huge handlebar mustache that curled all the way up his cheeks. \n\nBut anyway... no, I don't really think I have a hard time staying on topic. I usually get my point across just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind ever jumps between ideas in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, people seem to get what I'm saying just fine. I don't feel like my mind is jumping around or anything. Speaking of jumping, though, my cousin used to have this massive trampoline in his backyard. We'd go over there all the time when we were younger, and one summer I tried to do a double front flip and totally landed wrong. Snapped my arm right in half. They had to put pins in it and everything. The hospital food was the worst part, honestly. They gave me this green jello that tasted like... I don't know, dusty lime? My mom tried to sneak me in some fries from the drive-thru, but the head nurse caught her. That nurse had the loudest shoes, just squeaked every single time she walked down the linoleum hall. Squeak, squeak, squeak. Drove me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think I confuse people when I talk. I make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have trouble following your train of thought when you're explaining something?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. People usually just nod and agree with me, so I assume they're following along just fine. Like, just yesterday I was talking to the guy at the deli counter about the new vendor shipments we're dealing with at work, and he seemed totally on board. Speaking of the deli, they have this really good spicy mustard there now. It's got those little seeds in it? Reminds me of the mustard my uncle used to put on his hot dogs at the Fourth of July cookouts. He'd always burn the hot dogs, like, pitch black on the outside. We had to scrape the char off with a plastic knife. One time, my cousin accidentally swallowed a piece of the plastic knife because it snapped off in the meat. We had to take him to the emergency room, and we missed the fireworks. They were doing the big ones over the lake that year, too. You could hear the booms echoing off the water from miles away. It sounded like thunder. I love thunderstorms, honestly, the way the sky turns that weird purple color right before it pours. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think people have trouble following me. They get it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are disjointed or not flowing logically?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. My thoughts feel perfectly logical to me, they just flow from one thing to the next like water. Which is funny, because our water pressure at my apartment has been terrible lately. I called the landlord about it, but he never answers his phone. He's this older guy, always wears these bright yellow suspenders. Reminds me of a fireman or something. Actually, I wanted to be a fireman when I was a little kid. I had this plastic red helmet I wore everywhere, even to the grocery store. My mom hated it because the strap would always dig into my chin and leave a red mark. She used to try and hide it in the hall closet, but I always found it behind the winter coats. The closet always smelled like mothballs and wet wool. \n\nBut no, my thoughts aren't disjointed. They make perfect sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well, sometimes, yeah. Like, if I'm in the stockroom at work, or driving back from a vendor meeting, I'll hear this man's voice. It sounds like he's right behind me, just saying, "Move faster, Scott," or he'll start laughing at the inventory picks I just made. And I'll turn around, and there's nobody there. It's really weird. I don't know if he's trying to tell me something specific, really. He just says things like, "Move faster, Scott," or he just laughs at me. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what really made me come in is... things at work are just not right. I'm a wholesale buyer, and lately, I just can't shake the feeling that people at work—and even some of our vendors—are tracking my buys. Like, they're secretly messing with the launch numbers just to make me look stupid. My supervisor keeps pulling up the reports to show me they don't prove that, but I know what's happening. \n\nAnd honestly, the vendors are something else. There's this one guy I deal with, he always wears these ridiculously bright polo shirts. Neon orange, mostly. It reminds me of those traffic cones they used to put up near my old high school. They were doing all this construction on the main road for, like, three years straight when I was a teenager. I used to have to take this crazy detour through a neighborhood where this one guy had these little ceramic gnomes in his yard. Hundreds of them, just staring at the street. It was super weird, my buddy and I used to count them. But yeah... anyway, I just know they're altering my numbers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work and the vendors are actually out to get you, or is it just that you feel like something is off?\n\nPatient: No, they are definitely out to get me. It's not just a feeling. I know they're tracking my buys. They're going into the system and messing with the launch numbers just to make me look stupid. My supervisor keeps pulling up the reports, trying to show me that the numbers don't prove it, but I know what I'm seeing. \n\nAnd it's funny you mention feeling like something is off, because I was just at the grocery store the other day, and the cashier was looking at me exactly like my supervisor does. She had this little name tag that said \"Brenda,\" but it was crooked. I almost told her to fix it, but then I remembered how my mom used to hate it when people pointed out stuff like that. She was always so particular about her clothes. She used to iron everything, even my jeans when I was in high school. I hated it. They always felt so stiff, you know? Like cardboard. I used to try and break them in by doing squats in my bedroom before the bus came. But yeah, the numbers... they're definitely altering them. I'm not crazy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense when you're talking about what's going on at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think they make perfect sense. I know what I'm talking about when I say they're messing with my numbers. It's clear as day to me. But sometimes... I don't know, maybe I do get a little sidetracked. Like right now, I just remembered this one time I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the vendors, and I got totally distracted by this stain on his tie. It looked exactly like the shape of Florida. I went to Florida once when I was a kid. We drove all the way down to Orlando, and the AC in my dad's car broke halfway there. It was miserable. We had to roll all the windows down, and the wind was so loud we couldn't even hear the radio. My dad was playing this old cassette tape of the Eagles on repeat. I still can't listen to \"Hotel California\" without sweating. But anyway, yeah, I think my thoughts are connected. I just... I notice things, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay focused on one topic when talking about something important?\n\nPatient: I don't know, I haven't really noticed that being a problem. I feel like I stay pretty focused on the important stuff. Like, when I'm looking at the purchase orders, I'm locked in. Though, they just switched the supplier for our printer paper at the office, and it's so thin now. It feels exactly like the tissue paper my grandma used to stuff inside my birthday cards. She always put a crisp five-dollar bill in there. Never ten, always five. I used to take that five bucks down to the corner store and buy those little wax bottles with the colored syrup inside. You know the ones? You bite the top off... they tasted like pure plastic, honestly, but my buddy and I loved them. The guy who ran the store had this huge handlebar mustache that curled all the way up his cheeks. \n\nBut anyway... no, I don't really think I have a hard time staying on topic. I usually get my point across just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind ever jumps between ideas in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, people seem to get what I'm saying just fine. I don't feel like my mind is jumping around or anything. Speaking of jumping, though, my cousin used to have this massive trampoline in his backyard. We'd go over there all the time when we were younger, and one summer I tried to do a double front flip and totally landed wrong. Snapped my arm right in half. They had to put pins in it and everything. The hospital food was the worst part, honestly. They gave me this green jello that tasted like... I don't know, dusty lime? My mom tried to sneak me in some fries from the drive-thru, but the head nurse caught her. That nurse had the loudest shoes, just squeaked every single time she walked down the linoleum hall. Squeak, squeak, squeak. Drove me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think I confuse people when I talk. I make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have trouble following your train of thought when you're explaining something?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. People usually just nod and agree with me, so I assume they're following along just fine. Like, just yesterday I was talking to the guy at the deli counter about the new vendor shipments we're dealing with at work, and he seemed totally on board. Speaking of the deli, they have this really good spicy mustard there now. It's got those little seeds in it? Reminds me of the mustard my uncle used to put on his hot dogs at the Fourth of July cookouts. He'd always burn the hot dogs, like, pitch black on the outside. We had to scrape the char off with a plastic knife. One time, my cousin accidentally swallowed a piece of the plastic knife because it snapped off in the meat. We had to take him to the emergency room, and we missed the fireworks. They were doing the big ones over the lake that year, too. You could hear the booms echoing off the water from miles away. It sounded like thunder. I love thunderstorms, honestly, the way the sky turns that weird purple color right before it pours. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think people have trouble following me. They get it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are disjointed or not flowing logically?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. My thoughts feel perfectly logical to me, they just flow from one thing to the next like water. Which is funny, because our water pressure at my apartment has been terrible lately. I called the landlord about it, but he never answers his phone. He's this older guy, always wears these bright yellow suspenders. Reminds me of a fireman or something. Actually, I wanted to be a fireman when I was a little kid. I had this plastic red helmet I wore everywhere, even to the grocery store. My mom hated it because the strap would always dig into my chin and leave a red mark. She used to try and hide it in the hall closet, but I always found it behind the winter coats. The closet always smelled like mothballs and wet wool. \n\nBut no, my thoughts aren't disjointed. They make perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that you know are not really there?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes, yeah. Like, if I'm in the stockroom at work, or driving back from a vendor meeting, I'll hear this man's voice. It sounds like he's right behind me, just saying, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he'll start laughing at the inventory picks I just made. And I'll turn around, and there's nobody there. It's really weird. Speaking of driving, though, my car has been making this awful rattling noise lately. It sounds like a bunch of loose change shaking around in a tin can. I think it might be the muffler, but I'm not a car guy. My dad was a car guy, though. He used to spend every weekend in the garage, covered in grease, working on this old Chevy Nova. He loved that car more than anything. He'd always listen to classic rock on this beat-up boombox while he worked. I can still hear the bass from that thing vibrating through the floorboards into the kitchen. My mom used to complain about the noise all the time, especially when she was trying to watch her soap operas. She never missed an episode of General Hospital. \n\nBut yeah, I do hear that voice sometimes when nobody is around.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear when you're alone is trying to tell you something specific?\n\nPatient: I don't know if he's trying to tell me something specific, really. He just says things like, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he just laughs at me. Like he's judging the inventory picks I just made in the stockroom. It's always right behind me, too, like he's standing right over my shoulder. It happens when I'm driving back from vendor meetings, too. I hate those meetings, honestly. The vendors are always trying to push these new energy drinks on us. They come in these tall, skinny cans with neon green writing on them. They taste like battery acid, but people buy them like crazy. My buddy Dave drinks, like, three of them a day. I told him he's gonna give himself a heart attack, but he doesn't listen. Dave's stubborn like that. He once refused to wear a jacket in the middle of winter just to prove a point about cold tolerance. We were standing outside waiting for the bus, and it was snowing sideways. The snow was stinging my face like little needles. I hate the cold. It makes my joints ache, especially my knees. I messed up my knee playing basketball in high school. Landed wrong after a layup and just heard a pop. It swelled up to the size of a grapefruit. I had to wear this huge brace for months. It smelled terrible, like old sweat and neoprene. \n\nBut yeah, the voice just tells me to move faster or laughs at me. I don't know what it means.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's really weird. Speaking of driving, though, my car has been making this awful rattling noise lately. It sounds like a bunch of loose change shaking around in a tin can. I think it might be the muffler, but I'm not a car guy. My dad was a car guy, though. He used to spend every weekend in the garage, covered in grease, working on this old Chevy Nova. He loved that car more than anything. He'd always listen to classic rock on this beat-up boombox while he worked. She never missed an episode of General Hospital. \n\nBut yeah, I do hear that voice sometimes when nobody is around.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear when you're alone is trying to tell you something specific?\n\nPatient: I don't know if he's trying to tell me something specific, really. Dave's stubborn like that. He once refused to wear a jacket in the middle of winter just to prove a point about cold tolerance. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I haven't really thought about it like that. I don't know if they're connected. The voice is just... there. And the numbers, well, that's someone messing with my spreadsheets. Spreadsheets are such a pain anyway. We use this ancient software at work, it looks like it's from the nineties. The background is this awful gray color, and the font is so tiny you have to squint to read it. I told my manager we need to upgrade, but he says it's not in the budget. He's always talking about the budget. He has this little bald spot right on the crown of his head, and whenever he gets stressed about money, he rubs it. It's actually kind of funny to watch. He reminds me of my middle school math teacher, Mr. Henderson. He used to sweat so much during tests, he'd have these huge pit stains on his shirts. He always smelled like old coffee and peppermint breath mints. He was a nice guy, though. He let me retake a test once when I completely bombed it because I stayed up all night playing video games. I used to be obsessed with this one racing game, I'd play it for hours until my thumbs went numb. 

So, I don't know if the voice and the numbers are part of the same thing. I haven't really noticed a connection.

I mean, I guess? Sometimes I feel like I'm getting a massive amount done. Like, when I get really keyed up, I can't just sit there and handle one task. I'll be emailing three different vendors at the exact same time, making a whole new buy plan, and reorganizing all the product samples we have. Pacing ruins my shoes, though. I bought these leather slip-ons a couple months ago and the soles are already wearing thin on the heels. I usually just buy cheap shoes anyway because I drag my feet. My mom used to yell at me for scuffing up the kitchen floor when I was a teenager. We had this awful linoleum that was supposed to look like brick, but it was just orange squares. It was always peeling up in the corners near the sink. The sink always leaked, too. It was just a slow drip, drip, drip all night. Drove me crazy. I tried to fix it once with some duct tape, but it just made the water spray sideways all over the counter. I had to use all my mom's good towels to mop it up. She was so mad she didn't talk to me for two days. She was very particular about her towels. They had to be folded in thirds, not in half. 

But yeah, when I'm keyed up at work, I just have to keep moving and doing a bunch of things at once.

It's not just a feeling. I know they're tracking my buys. They're going into the system and messing with the launch numbers just to make me look stupid. My supervisor keeps pulling up the reports, trying to show me that the numbers don't prove it, but I know what I'm seeing. \n\nAnd it's funny you mention feeling like something is off, because I was just at the grocery store the other day, and the cashier was looking at me exactly like my supervisor does. Like right now, I just remembered this one time I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the vendors, and I got totally distracted by this stain on his tie. It looked exactly like the shape of Florida. I went to Florida once when I was a kid. We drove all the way down to Orlando, and the AC in my dad's car broke halfway there. It was miserable. We had to roll all the windows down, and the wind was so loud we couldn't even hear the radio. My dad was playing this old cassette tape of the Eagles on repeat. I still can't listen to \"Hotel California\" without sweating. But anyway, yeah, I think my thoughts are connected. I just... I notice things, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay focused on one topic when talking about something important?\n\nPatient: I don't know, I haven't really noticed that being a problem. I feel like I stay pretty focused on the important stuff. Like, when I'm looking at the purchase orders, I'm locked in. Though, they just switched the supplier for our printer paper at the office, and it's so thin now. It feels exactly like the tissue paper my grandma used to stuff inside my birthday cards. She always put a crisp five-dollar bill in there. Never ten, always five. I used to take that five bucks down to the corner store and buy those little wax bottles with the colored syrup inside. You know the ones? You bite the top off... they tasted like pure plastic, honestly, but my buddy and I loved them. The guy who ran the store had this huge handlebar mustache that curled all the way up his cheeks. \n\nBut anyway... no, I don't really think I have a hard time staying on topic. I usually get my point across just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind ever jumps between ideas in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, people seem to get what I'm saying just fine. I don't feel like my mind is jumping around or anything. Speaking of jumping, though, my cousin used to have this massive trampoline in his backyard. We'd go over there all the time when we were younger, and one summer I tried to do a double front flip and totally landed wrong. Snapped my arm right in half. They had to put pins in it and everything. The hospital food was the worst part, honestly. They gave me this green jello that tasted like... I don't know, dusty lime? My mom tried to sneak me in some fries from the drive-thru, but the head nurse caught her. That nurse had the loudest shoes, just squeaked every single time she walked down the linoleum hall. I make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have trouble following your train of thought when you're explaining something?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. People usually just nod and agree with me, so I assume they're following along just fine. Like, just yesterday I was talking to the guy at the deli counter about the new vendor shipments we're dealing with at work, and he seemed totally on board. Speaking of the deli, they have this really good spicy mustard there now. It's got those little seeds in it? Reminds me of the mustard my uncle used to put on his hot dogs at the Fourth of July cookouts. He'd always burn the hot dogs, like, pitch black on the outside. We had to scrape the char off with a plastic knife. One time, my cousin accidentally swallowed a piece of the plastic knife because it snapped off in the meat. We had to take him to the emergency room, and we missed the fireworks. They were doing the big ones over the lake that year, too. You could hear the booms echoing off the water from miles away. It sounded like thunder. I love thunderstorms, honestly, the way the sky turns that weird purple color right before it pours. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think people have trouble following me. They get it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are disjointed or not flowing logically?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. My thoughts feel perfectly logical to me, they just flow from one thing to the next like water. Which is funny, because our water pressure at my apartment has been terrible lately. I called the landlord about it, but he never answers his phone. He's this older guy, always wears these bright yellow suspenders. Reminds me of a fireman or something. Actually, I wanted to be a fireman when I was a little kid. I had this plastic red helmet I wore everywhere, even to the grocery store. I'm not crazy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense when you're talking about what's going on at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think they make perfect sense. Like right now, I just remembered this one time I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the vendors, and I got totally distracted by this stain on his tie. It looked exactly like the shape of Florida. I went to Florida once when I was a kid. We drove all the way down to Orlando, and the AC in my dad's car broke halfway there. It was miserable. We had to roll all the windows down, and the wind was so loud we couldn't even hear the radio. My dad was playing this old cassette tape of the Eagles on repeat. I still can't listen to \"Hotel California\" without sweating. But anyway, yeah, I think my thoughts are connected. I just... I notice things, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay focused on one topic when talking about something important?\n\nPatient: I don't know, I haven't really noticed that being a problem. Though, they just switched the supplier for our printer paper at the office, and it's so thin now. It feels exactly like the tissue paper my grandma used to stuff inside my birthday cards. She always put a crisp five-dollar bill in there. Never ten, always five. I used to take that five bucks down to the corner store and buy those little wax bottles with the colored syrup inside. You know the ones? You bite the top off... they tasted like pure plastic, honestly, but my buddy and I loved them. The guy who ran the store had this huge handlebar mustache that curled all the way up his cheeks. \n\nBut anyway... no, I don't really think I have a hard time staying on topic. I usually get my point across just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind ever jumps between ideas in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, people seem to get what I'm saying just fine. I don't feel like my mind is jumping around or anything. Speaking of jumping, though, my cousin used to have this massive trampoline in his backyard. We'd go over there all the time when we were younger, and one summer I tried to do a double front flip and totally landed wrong. Snapped my arm right in half. They had to put pins in it and everything. The hospital food was the worst part, honestly. They gave me this green jello that tasted like... I don't know, dusty lime? My mom tried to sneak me in some fries from the drive-thru, but the head nurse caught her. That nurse had the loudest shoes, just squeaked every single time she walked down the linoleum hall. Squeak, squeak, squeak. Drove me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think I confuse people when I talk. I make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have trouble following your train of thought when you're explaining something?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. People usually just nod and agree with me, so I assume they're following along just fine. Like, just yesterday I was talking to the guy at the deli counter about the new vendor shipments we're dealing with at work, and he seemed totally on board. Speaking of the deli, they have this really good spicy mustard there now. It's got those little seeds in it? Reminds me of the mustard my uncle used to put on his hot dogs at the Fourth of July cookouts. He'd always burn the hot dogs, like, pitch black on the outside. We had to scrape the char off with a plastic knife. One time, my cousin accidentally swallowed a piece of the plastic knife because it snapped off in the meat. We had to take him to the emergency room, and we missed the fireworks. They were doing the big ones over the lake that year, too. You could hear the booms echoing off the water from miles away. It sounded like thunder. I love thunderstorms, honestly, the way the sky turns that weird purple color right before it pours. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think people have trouble following me. They get it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are disjointed or not flowing logically?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. My thoughts feel perfectly logical to me, they just flow from one thing to the next like water. Which is funny, because our water pressure at my apartment has been terrible lately. I called the landlord about it, but he never answers his phone. He's this older guy, always wears these bright yellow suspenders. Reminds me of a fireman or something. Actually, I wanted to be a fireman when I was a little kid. I had this plastic red helmet I wore everywhere, even to the grocery store. My mom hated it because the strap would always dig into my chin and leave a red mark. She used to try and hide it in the hall closet, but I always found it behind the winter coats. The closet always smelled like mothballs and wet wool. \n\nBut no, my thoughts aren't disjointed. It's really weird. Speaking of driving, though, my car has been making this awful rattling noise lately. It sounds like a bunch of loose change shaking around in a tin can. I think it might be the muffler, but I'm not a car guy. My dad was a car guy, though. He used to spend every weekend in the garage, covered in grease, working on this old Chevy Nova. He loved that car more than anything. He'd always listen to classic rock on this beat-up boombox while he worked. I can still hear the bass from that thing vibrating through the floorboards into the kitchen. My mom used to complain about the noise all the time, especially when she was trying to watch her soap operas. She never missed an episode of General Hospital. \n\nBut yeah, I do hear that voice sometimes when nobody is around.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear when you're alone is trying to tell you something specific?\n\nPatient: I don't know if he's trying to tell me something specific, really. Like, just yesterday I was talking to the guy at the deli counter about the new vendor shipments we're dealing with at work, and he seemed totally on board. Speaking of the deli, they have this really good spicy mustard there now. It's got those little seeds in it? Reminds me of the mustard my uncle used to put on his hot dogs at the Fourth of July cookouts. He'd always burn the hot dogs, like, pitch black on the outside. We had to scrape the char off with a plastic knife. One time, my cousin accidentally swallowed a piece of the plastic knife because it snapped off in the meat. We had to take him to the emergency room, and we missed the fireworks. They were doing the big ones over the lake that year, too. You could hear the booms echoing off the water from miles away. It sounded like thunder. I love thunderstorms, honestly, the way the sky turns that weird purple color right before it pours. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think people have trouble following me. They get it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are disjointed or not flowing logically?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. My thoughts feel perfectly logical to me, they just flow from one thing to the next like water. Which is funny, because our water pressure at my apartment has been terrible lately. I called the landlord about it, but he never answers his phone. He's this older guy, always wears these bright yellow suspenders. Reminds me of a fireman or something. Actually, I wanted to be a fireman when I was a little kid. I had this plastic red helmet I wore everywhere, even to the grocery store. My mom hated it because the strap would always dig into my chin and leave a red mark. She used to try and hide it in the hall closet, but I always found it behind the winter coats. The closet always smelled like mothballs and wet wool. \n\nBut no, my thoughts aren't disjointed. They make perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that you know are not really there?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes, yeah. Like, if I'm in the stockroom at work, or driving back from a vendor meeting, I'll hear this man's voice. It sounds like he's right behind me, just saying, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he'll start laughing at the inventory picks I just made. And I'll turn around, and there's nobody there. It's really weird. Speaking of driving, though, my car has been making this awful rattling noise lately. It sounds like a bunch of loose change shaking around in a tin can. I think it might be the muffler, but I'm not a car guy. My dad was a car guy, though. He used to spend every weekend in the garage, covered in grease, working on this old Chevy Nova. He loved that car more than anything. He'd always listen to classic rock on this beat-up boombox while he worked. I can still hear the bass from that thing vibrating through the floorboards into the kitchen. My mom used to complain about the noise all the time, especially when she was trying to watch her soap operas. She never missed an episode of General Hospital. \n\nBut yeah, I do hear that voice sometimes when nobody is around.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear when you're alone is trying to tell you something specific?\n\nPatient: I don't know if he's trying to tell me something specific, really. He just says things like, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he just laughs at me. Like he's judging the inventory picks I just made in the stockroom. It's always right behind me, too, like he's standing right over my shoulder. It happens when I'm driving back from vendor meetings, too. I hate those meetings, honestly. The vendors are always trying to push these new energy drinks on us. They come in these tall, skinny cans with neon green writing on them. They taste like battery acid, but people buy them like crazy. My buddy Dave drinks, like, three of them a day. I told him he's gonna give himself a heart attack, but he doesn't listen. Dave's stubborn like that. He once refused to wear a jacket in the middle of winter just to prove a point about cold tolerance. We were standing outside waiting for the bus, and it was snowing sideways. The snow was stinging my face like little needles. I hate the cold. It makes my joints ache, especially my knees. I messed up my knee playing basketball in high school. Landed wrong after a layup and just heard a pop. It swelled up to the size of a grapefruit. I had to wear this huge brace for months. It smelled terrible, like old sweat and neoprene. \n\nBut yeah, the voice just tells me to move faster or laughs at me. He has this little bald spot right on the crown of his head, and whenever he gets stressed about money, he rubs it. It's actually kind of funny to watch. He reminds me of my middle school math teacher, Mr. Henderson. He used to sweat so much during tests, he'd have these huge pit stains on his shirts. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know if they're disjointed, exactly. It's more like... people tell me I talk over them in meetings. Like, once I start explaining a new drop or a big deal I'm trying to work out, the words just come out so fast. I get going and I barely even notice anyone trying to jump in or ask a question. It's like I have to get it all out before I forget it. 

I actually got in trouble for that in high school once. We had this debate class, and I was supposed to be arguing about... I think it was school uniforms? Yeah, school uniforms. I was so against them. I mean, wearing the same thing every day is just so boring. I like wearing my vintage band tees. I have this really cool Nirvana shirt I found at a thrift store. It's perfectly faded and has this tiny hole near the collar, but that just makes it look better, you know? Thrift stores are the best. You never know what you're gonna find. I found a working Atari console once for like ten bucks. Best day ever. We hooked it up to this old tube TV my grandma gave me. The picture was always a little fuzzy, and you had to smack the side of the TV sometimes to fix the color, but playing Pac-Man on it was awesome. 

So yeah, I guess I just talk really fast when it's important, and people can't always get a word in edgewise.

I used to have to take this crazy detour through a neighborhood where this one guy had these little ceramic gnomes in his yard. Hundreds of them, just staring at the street. It was super weird, my buddy and I used to count them. But yeah... anyway, I just know they're altering my numbers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work and the vendors are actually out to get you, or is it just that you feel like something is off?\n\nPatient: No, they are definitely out to get me. It's not just a feeling. I know they're tracking my buys. They're going into the system and messing with the launch numbers just to make me look stupid. My supervisor keeps pulling up the reports, trying to show me that the numbers don't prove it, but I know what I'm seeing. \n\nAnd it's funny you mention feeling like something is off, because I was just at the grocery store the other day, and the cashier was looking at me exactly like my supervisor does. She had this little name tag that said \"Brenda,\" but it was crooked. I almost told her to fix it, but then I remembered how my mom used to hate it when people pointed out stuff like that. She was always so particular about her clothes. She used to iron everything, even my jeans when I was in high school. I hated it. They always felt so stiff, you know? Like cardboard. I used to try and break them in by doing squats in my bedroom before the bus came. But yeah, the numbers... they're definitely altering them. I'm not crazy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense when you're talking about what's going on at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think they make perfect sense. I know what I'm talking about when I say they're messing with my numbers. It's clear as day to me. But sometimes... I don't know, maybe I do get a little sidetracked. Like right now, I just remembered this one time I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the vendors, and I got totally distracted by this stain on his tie. It looked exactly like the shape of Florida. I went to Florida once when I was a kid. We drove all the way down to Orlando, and the AC in my dad's car broke halfway there. It was miserable. We had to roll all the windows down, and the wind was so loud we couldn't even hear the radio. My dad was playing this old cassette tape of the Eagles on repeat. I still can't listen to \"Hotel California\" without sweating. But anyway, yeah, I think my thoughts are connected. I just... I notice things, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay focused on one topic when talking about something important?\n\nPatient: I don't know, I haven't really noticed that being a problem. I feel like I stay pretty focused on the important stuff. Like, when I'm looking at the purchase orders, I'm locked in. Though, they just switched the supplier for our printer paper at the office, and it's so thin now. It feels exactly like the tissue paper my grandma used to stuff inside my birthday cards. She always put a crisp five-dollar bill in there. Never ten, always five. I used to take that five bucks down to the corner store and buy those little wax bottles with the colored syrup inside. You know the ones? You bite the top off... they tasted like pure plastic, honestly, but my buddy and I loved them. The guy who ran the store had this huge handlebar mustache that curled all the way up his cheeks. \n\nBut anyway... no, I don't really think I have a hard time staying on topic. I usually get my point across just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind ever jumps between ideas in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, people seem to get what I'm saying just fine. I don't feel like my mind is jumping around or anything. Speaking of jumping, though, my cousin used to have this massive trampoline in his backyard. We'd go over there all the time when we were younger, and one summer I tried to do a double front flip and totally landed wrong. Snapped my arm right in half. They had to put pins in it and everything. The hospital food was the worst part, honestly. They gave me this green jello that tasted like... I don't know, dusty lime? My mom tried to sneak me in some fries from the drive-thru, but the head nurse caught her. That nurse had the loudest shoes, just squeaked every single time she walked down the linoleum hall. Squeak, squeak, squeak. Drove me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think I confuse people when I talk. I make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have trouble following your train of thought when you're explaining something?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. People usually just nod and agree with me, so I assume they're following along just fine. Like, just yesterday I was talking to the guy at the deli counter about the new vendor shipments we're dealing with at work, and he seemed totally on board. Speaking of the deli, they have this really good spicy mustard there now. It's got those little seeds in it? Reminds me of the mustard my uncle used to put on his hot dogs at the Fourth of July cookouts. He'd always burn the hot dogs, like, pitch black on the outside. We had to scrape the char off with a plastic knife. One time, my cousin accidentally swallowed a piece of the plastic knife because it snapped off in the meat. We had to take him to the emergency room, and we missed the fireworks. They were doing the big ones over the lake that year, too. You could hear the booms echoing off the water from miles away. It sounded like thunder. I love thunderstorms, honestly, the way the sky turns that weird purple color right before it pours. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think people have trouble following me. They get it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are disjointed or not flowing logically?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. My thoughts feel perfectly logical to me, they just flow from one thing to the next like water. Which is funny, because our water pressure at my apartment has been terrible lately. I called the landlord about it, but he never answers his phone. He's this older guy, always wears these bright yellow suspenders. Reminds me of a fireman or something. Actually, I wanted to be a fireman when I was a little kid. I had this plastic red helmet I wore everywhere, even to the grocery store. My mom hated it because the strap would always dig into my chin and leave a red mark. She used to try and hide it in the hall closet, but I always found it behind the winter coats. The closet always smelled like mothballs and wet wool. \n\nBut no, my thoughts aren't disjointed. They make perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that you know are not really there?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes, yeah. Like, if I'm in the stockroom at work, or driving back from a vendor meeting, I'll hear this man's voice. It sounds like he's right behind me, just saying, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he'll start laughing at the inventory picks I just made. And I'll turn around, and there's nobody there. It's really weird. Speaking of driving, though, my car has been making this awful rattling noise lately. It sounds like a bunch of loose change shaking around in a tin can. I think it might be the muffler, but I'm not a car guy. My dad was a car guy, though. He used to spend every weekend in the garage, covered in grease, working on this old Chevy Nova. He loved that car more than anything. He'd always listen to classic rock on this beat-up boombox while he worked. I can still hear the bass from that thing vibrating through the floorboards into the kitchen. My mom used to complain about the noise all the time, especially when she was trying to watch her soap operas. She never missed an episode of General Hospital. \n\nBut yeah, I do hear that voice sometimes when nobody is around.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear when you're alone is trying to tell you something specific?\n\nPatient: I don't know if he's trying to tell me something specific, really. He just says things like, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he just laughs at me. Like he's judging the inventory picks I just made in the stockroom. It's always right behind me, too, like he's standing right over my shoulder. It happens when I'm driving back from vendor meetings, too. I hate those meetings, honestly. The vendors are always trying to push these new energy drinks on us. They come in these tall, skinny cans with neon green writing on them. They taste like battery acid, but people buy them like crazy. My buddy Dave drinks, like, three of them a day. I told him he's gonna give himself a heart attack, but he doesn't listen. Dave's stubborn like that. He once refused to wear a jacket in the middle of winter just to prove a point about cold tolerance. We were standing outside waiting for the bus, and it was snowing sideways. The snow was stinging my face like little needles. I hate the cold. It makes my joints ache, especially my knees. I messed up my knee playing basketball in high school. Landed wrong after a layup and just heard a pop. It swelled up to the size of a grapefruit. I had to wear this huge brace for months. It smelled terrible, like old sweat and neoprene. \n\nBut yeah, the voice just tells me to move faster or laughs at me. I don't know what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice you hear and the people altering your numbers at work are all part of the same thing happening to you?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about it like that. I don't know if they're connected. The voice is just... there. And the numbers, well, that's someone messing with my spreadsheets. Spreadsheets are such a pain anyway. We use this ancient software at work, it looks like it's from the nineties. The background is this awful gray color, and the font is so tiny you have to squint to read it. I told my manager we need to upgrade, but he says it's not in the budget. He's always talking about the budget. He has this little bald spot right on the crown of his head, and whenever he gets stressed about money, he rubs it. It's actually kind of funny to watch. He reminds me of my middle school math teacher, Mr. Henderson. He used to sweat so much during tests, he'd have these huge pit stains on his shirts. He always smelled like old coffee and peppermint breath mints. He was a nice guy, though. He let me retake a test once when I completely bombed it because I stayed up all night playing video games. I used to be obsessed with this one racing game, I'd play it for hours until my thumbs went numb. \n\nSo, I don't know if the voice and the numbers are part of the same thing. I haven't really noticed a connection.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts and actions are helping you achieve what you want to accomplish at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, I guess? Sometimes I feel like I'm getting a massive amount done. Like, when I get really keyed up, I can't just sit there and handle one task. I'll be emailing three different vendors at the exact same time, making a whole new buy plan, and reorganizing all the product samples we have. I'll just be pacing the office the whole time, because I feel like if I stop moving, I'll completely lose the thread of what I'm doing. \n\nPacing ruins my shoes, though. I bought these leather slip-ons a couple months ago and the soles are already wearing thin on the heels. I usually just buy cheap shoes anyway because I drag my feet. My mom used to yell at me for scuffing up the kitchen floor when I was a teenager. We had this awful linoleum that was supposed to look like brick, but it was just orange squares. It was always peeling up in the corners near the sink. The sink always leaked, too. It was just a slow drip, drip, drip all night. Drove me crazy. I tried to fix it once with some duct tape, but it just made the water spray sideways all over the counter. I had to use all my mom's good towels to mop it up. She was so mad she didn't talk to me for two days. She was very particular about her towels. They had to be folded in thirds, not in half. \n\nBut yeah, when I'm keyed up at work, I just have to keep moving and doing a bunch of things at once.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are organized when you're talking about multiple things at once?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I guess they make sense to me at the time. It's just that everything is happening so fast in my head, you know? Like, I'll be talking about the buy plan and then I'll remember the product samples, and it all feels connected right then. It's kind of like how a spider web works, all these little threads attaching to each other. I used to be terrified of spiders when I was a kid. My older brother, he used to catch these huge wolf spiders in the backyard and put them in my bed. He thought it was hilarious. He was always doing stuff like that, pulling pranks. Once he put Saran wrap over the toilet bowl. That was a disaster. My dad made him scrub the entire bathroom with a toothbrush after that. The bathroom always smelled like bleach anyway, because my dad was kind of a neat freak. He used to vacuum the living room every single day. The vacuum cleaner was this heavy, old Hoover that sounded like an airplane taking off. It drove our dog, Buster, absolutely insane. He'd bark at it and try to bite the wheels. Buster was a golden retriever mix, best dog ever. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I don't know if my thoughts are organized or not. They just come out however they come out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are disjointed when you're explaining something important to someone?\n\nPatient: I don't know if they're disjointed, exactly. It's more like... people tell me I talk over them in meetings. Like, once I start explaining a new drop or a big deal I'm trying to work out, the words just come out so fast. I get going and I barely even notice anyone trying to jump in or ask a question. It's like I have to get it all out before I forget it. \n\nI actually got in trouble for that in high school once. We had this debate class, and I was supposed to be arguing about... I think it was school uniforms? Yeah, school uniforms. I was so against them. I mean, wearing the same thing every day is just so boring. I like wearing my vintage band tees. I have this really cool Nirvana shirt I found at a thrift store. It's perfectly faded and has this tiny hole near the collar, but that just makes it look better, you know? Thrift stores are the best. You never know what you're gonna find. I found a working Atari console once for like ten bucks. Best day ever. We hooked it up to this old tube TV my grandma gave me. The picture was always a little fuzzy, and you had to smack the side of the TV sometimes to fix the color, but playing Pac-Man on it was awesome. \n\nSo yeah, I guess I just talk really fast when it's important, and people can't always get a word in edgewise.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I don't really know if they're disorganized outside of work. I haven't really thought about it, honestly. Like, when I'm just hanging out with my friends, we mostly just talk about music or whatever. Actually, I wanted to be a fireman when I was a little kid. I had this plastic red helmet I wore everywhere, even to the grocery store. I told my manager we need to upgrade, but he says it's not in the budget. He's always talking about the budget. He has this little bald spot right on the crown of his head, and whenever he gets stressed about money, he rubs it. It's actually kind of funny to watch. I haven't really noticed a connection.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts and actions are helping you achieve what you want to accomplish at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, I guess? I'll be emailing three different vendors at the exact same time, making a whole new buy plan, and reorganizing all the product samples we have. I'll just be pacing the office the whole time, because I feel like if I stop moving, I'll completely lose the thread of what I'm doing. \n\nPacing ruins my shoes, though. I bought these leather slip-ons a couple months ago and the soles are already wearing thin on the heels. I usually just buy cheap shoes anyway because I drag my feet. My mom used to yell at me for scuffing up the kitchen floor when I was a teenager. We had this awful linoleum that was supposed to look like brick, but it was just orange squares. It was always peeling up in the corners near the sink. The sink always leaked, too. It was just a slow drip, drip, drip all night. Drove me crazy. It's kind of like how a spider web works, all these little threads attaching to each other. I used to be terrified of spiders when I was a kid. They were doing all this construction on the main road for, like, three years straight when I was a teenager. I used to have to take this crazy detour through a neighborhood where this one guy had these little ceramic gnomes in his yard. Hundreds of them, just staring at the street. It was super weird, my buddy and I used to count them. But yeah... anyway, I just know they're altering my numbers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work and the vendors are actually out to get you, or is it just that you feel like something is off?\n\nPatient: No, they are definitely out to get me. It's not just a feeling. I know they're tracking my buys. They're going into the system and messing with the launch numbers just to make me look stupid. My supervisor keeps pulling up the reports, trying to show me that the numbers don't prove it, but I know what I'm seeing. \n\nAnd it's funny you mention feeling like something is off, because I was just at the grocery store the other day, and the cashier was looking at me exactly like my supervisor does. She had this little name tag that said \"Brenda,\" but it was crooked. I almost told her to fix it, but then I remembered how my mom used to hate it when people pointed out stuff like that. She was always so particular about her clothes. She used to iron everything, even my jeans when I was in high school. I hated it. They always felt so stiff, you know? Like cardboard. I used to try and break them in by doing squats in my bedroom before the bus came. But yeah, the numbers... they're definitely altering them. I'm not crazy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense when you're talking about what's going on at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think they make perfect sense. I know what I'm talking about when I say they're messing with my numbers. It's clear as day to me. But sometimes... I don't know, maybe I do get a little sidetracked. Like right now, I just remembered this one time I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the vendors, and I got totally distracted by this stain on his tie. It looked exactly like the shape of Florida. I went to Florida once when I was a kid. We drove all the way down to Orlando, and the AC in my dad's car broke halfway there. It was miserable. We had to roll all the windows down, and the wind was so loud we couldn't even hear the radio. My dad was playing this old cassette tape of the Eagles on repeat. I still can't listen to \"Hotel California\" without sweating. But anyway, yeah, I think my thoughts are connected. I just... I notice things, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay focused on one topic when talking about something important?\n\nPatient: I don't know, I haven't really noticed that being a problem. I feel like I stay pretty focused on the important stuff. Like, when I'm looking at the purchase orders, I'm locked in. Though, they just switched the supplier for our printer paper at the office, and it's so thin now. It feels exactly like the tissue paper my grandma used to stuff inside my birthday cards. She always put a crisp five-dollar bill in there. Never ten, always five. I used to take that five bucks down to the corner store and buy those little wax bottles with the colored syrup inside. You know the ones? You bite the top off... they tasted like pure plastic, honestly, but my buddy and I loved them. The guy who ran the store had this huge handlebar mustache that curled all the way up his cheeks. \n\nBut anyway... no, I don't really think I have a hard time staying on topic. I usually get my point across just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind ever jumps between ideas in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, people seem to get what I'm saying just fine. I don't feel like my mind is jumping around or anything. Speaking of jumping, though, my cousin used to have this massive trampoline in his backyard. We'd go over there all the time when we were younger, and one summer I tried to do a double front flip and totally landed wrong. Snapped my arm right in half. They had to put pins in it and everything. The hospital food was the worst part, honestly. They gave me this green jello that tasted like... I don't know, dusty lime? My mom tried to sneak me in some fries from the drive-thru, but the head nurse caught her. That nurse had the loudest shoes, just squeaked every single time she walked down the linoleum hall. Squeak, squeak, squeak. Drove me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think I confuse people when I talk. I make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have trouble following your train of thought when you're explaining something?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. People usually just nod and agree with me, so I assume they're following along just fine. Speaking of the deli, they have this really good spicy mustard there now. It's got those little seeds in it? Reminds me of the mustard my uncle used to put on his hot dogs at the Fourth of July cookouts. He'd always burn the hot dogs, like, pitch black on the outside. We had to scrape the char off with a plastic knife. One time, my cousin accidentally swallowed a piece of the plastic knife because it snapped off in the meat. We had to take him to the emergency room, and we missed the fireworks. They were doing the big ones over the lake that year, too. You could hear the booms echoing off the water from miles away. It sounded like thunder. I love thunderstorms, honestly, the way the sky turns that weird purple color right before it pours. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think people have trouble following me. They get it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are disjointed or not flowing logically?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. My thoughts feel perfectly logical to me, they just flow from one thing to the next like water. Which is funny, because our water pressure at my apartment has been terrible lately. I called the landlord about it, but he never answers his phone. He's this older guy, always wears these bright yellow suspenders. Reminds me of a fireman or something. Actually, I wanted to be a fireman when I was a little kid. I had this plastic red helmet I wore everywhere, even to the grocery store. My mom hated it because the strap would always dig into my chin and leave a red mark. She used to try and hide it in the hall closet, but I always found it behind the winter coats. The closet always smelled like mothballs and wet wool. \n\nBut no, my thoughts aren't disjointed. They make perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that you know are not really there?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes, yeah. Like, if I'm in the stockroom at work, or driving back from a vendor meeting, I'll hear this man's voice. It sounds like he's right behind me, just saying, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he'll start laughing at the inventory picks I just made. And I'll turn around, and there's nobody there. He just says things like, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he just laughs at me. Like he's judging the inventory picks I just made in the stockroom. It's always right behind me, too, like he's standing right over my shoulder. It happens when I'm driving back from vendor meetings, too. I hate those meetings, honestly. The vendors are always trying to push these new energy drinks on us. They come in these tall, skinny cans with neon green writing on them. They taste like battery acid, but people buy them like crazy. My buddy Dave drinks, like, three of them a day. I told him he's gonna give himself a heart attack, but he doesn't listen. Dave's stubborn like that. He once refused to wear a jacket in the middle of winter just to prove a point about cold tolerance. We were standing outside waiting for the bus, and it was snowing sideways. The snow was stinging my face like little needles. I hate the cold. It makes my joints ache, especially my knees. I messed up my knee playing basketball in high school. Landed wrong after a layup and just heard a pop. It swelled up to the size of a grapefruit. I had to wear this huge brace for months. It smelled terrible, like old sweat and neoprene. \n\nBut yeah, the voice just tells me to move faster or laughs at me. I don't know what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice you hear and the people altering your numbers at work are all part of the same thing happening to you?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about it like that. We use this ancient software at work, it looks like it's from the nineties. The background is this awful gray color, and the font is so tiny you have to squint to read it. He reminds me of my middle school math teacher, Mr. Henderson. He used to sweat so much during tests, he'd have these huge pit stains on his shirts. He always smelled like old coffee and peppermint breath mints. He was a nice guy, though. He let me retake a test once when I completely bombed it because I stayed up all night playing video games. I used to be obsessed with this one racing game, I'd play it for hours until my thumbs went numb. \n\nSo, I don't know if the voice and the numbers are part of the same thing. I haven't really noticed a connection.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts and actions are helping you achieve what you want to accomplish at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, I guess? Sometimes I feel like I'm getting a massive amount done. Like, when I get really keyed up, I can't just sit there and handle one task. I'll be emailing three different vendors at the exact same time, making a whole new buy plan, and reorganizing all the product samples we have. I'll just be pacing the office the whole time, because I feel like if I stop moving, I'll completely lose the thread of what I'm doing. \n\nPacing ruins my shoes, though. I bought these leather slip-ons a couple months ago and the soles are already wearing thin on the heels. I usually just buy cheap shoes anyway because I drag my feet. She was so mad she didn't talk to me for two days. She was very particular about her towels. Once he put Saran wrap over the toilet bowl. That was a disaster. My dad made him scrub the entire bathroom with a toothbrush after that. Buster was a golden retriever mix, best dog ever. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I don't know if my thoughts are organized or not. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, I don't know if it messes up my thoughts when I'm talking to people, because I mostly just hear it when I'm by myself. Like, sometimes when I'm in the stockroom or driving back from a vendor meeting, I'll hear this man's voice. It sounds like it's coming from right behind me, just saying, "Move faster, Scott," or he's laughing at my picks. And I'll turn around, and nobody's there. 

It really freaked me out the first time it happened in my car, because my car is tiny. It's this little hatchback I got a few years ago. The air conditioning in it is totally busted, so I have to drive with the windows down all summer. Driving with the windows down on the highway is the worst, though, because the wind just whips your hair right into your eyes. I need to get a haircut, actually. My barber, Tony, he's been cutting my hair since I was like ten. He always gives me a lollipop when I leave, even though I'm twenty-two. It's always the cherry ones. Cherry is okay, but blue raspberry is definitely the best flavor for candy. Jolly Ranchers, especially. I could eat a whole bag of blue raspberry Jolly Ranchers until my tongue turns completely blue. 

But yeah, I don't know if the voice makes my thoughts disorganized or anything.

She had this little name tag that said \"Brenda,\" but it was crooked. I almost told her to fix it, but then I remembered how my mom used to hate it when people pointed out stuff like that. She was always so particular about her clothes. She used to iron everything, even my jeans when I was in high school. I hated it. They always felt so stiff, you know? Like cardboard. I used to try and break them in by doing squats in my bedroom before the bus came. But yeah, the numbers... they're definitely altering them. I'm not crazy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense when you're talking about what's going on at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think they make perfect sense. I know what I'm talking about when I say they're messing with my numbers. It's clear as day to me. But sometimes... I don't know, maybe I do get a little sidetracked. Like right now, I just remembered this one time I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the vendors, and I got totally distracted by this stain on his tie. It looked exactly like the shape of Florida. I went to Florida once when I was a kid. We drove all the way down to Orlando, and the AC in my dad's car broke halfway there. It was miserable. We had to roll all the windows down, and the wind was so loud we couldn't even hear the radio. My dad was playing this old cassette tape of the Eagles on repeat. I still can't listen to \"Hotel California\" without sweating. But anyway, yeah, I think my thoughts are connected. I just... I notice things, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay focused on one topic when talking about something important?\n\nPatient: I don't know, I haven't really noticed that being a problem. I feel like I stay pretty focused on the important stuff. Like, when I'm looking at the purchase orders, I'm locked in. Though, they just switched the supplier for our printer paper at the office, and it's so thin now. It feels exactly like the tissue paper my grandma used to stuff inside my birthday cards. She always put a crisp five-dollar bill in there. Never ten, always five. I used to take that five bucks down to the corner store and buy those little wax bottles with the colored syrup inside. You know the ones? You bite the top off... they tasted like pure plastic, honestly, but my buddy and I loved them. The guy who ran the store had this huge handlebar mustache that curled all the way up his cheeks. \n\nBut anyway... no, I don't really think I have a hard time staying on topic. I usually get my point across just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind ever jumps between ideas in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, people seem to get what I'm saying just fine. I don't feel like my mind is jumping around or anything. Speaking of jumping, though, my cousin used to have this massive trampoline in his backyard. We'd go over there all the time when we were younger, and one summer I tried to do a double front flip and totally landed wrong. Snapped my arm right in half. They had to put pins in it and everything. The hospital food was the worst part, honestly. They gave me this green jello that tasted like... I don't know, dusty lime? My mom tried to sneak me in some fries from the drive-thru, but the head nurse caught her. That nurse had the loudest shoes, just squeaked every single time she walked down the linoleum hall. Squeak, squeak, squeak. Drove me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think I confuse people when I talk. I make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have trouble following your train of thought when you're explaining something?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. People usually just nod and agree with me, so I assume they're following along just fine. Speaking of the deli, they have this really good spicy mustard there now. It's got those little seeds in it? Reminds me of the mustard my uncle used to put on his hot dogs at the Fourth of July cookouts. He'd always burn the hot dogs, like, pitch black on the outside. We had to scrape the char off with a plastic knife. One time, my cousin accidentally swallowed a piece of the plastic knife because it snapped off in the meat. We had to take him to the emergency room, and we missed the fireworks. They were doing the big ones over the lake that year, too. You could hear the booms echoing off the water from miles away. It sounded like thunder. I love thunderstorms, honestly, the way the sky turns that weird purple color right before it pours. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't think people have trouble following me. They get it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are disjointed or not flowing logically?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. My thoughts feel perfectly logical to me, they just flow from one thing to the next like water. Which is funny, because our water pressure at my apartment has been terrible lately. I called the landlord about it, but he never answers his phone. He's this older guy, always wears these bright yellow suspenders. Reminds me of a fireman or something. Actually, I wanted to be a fireman when I was a little kid. I had this plastic red helmet I wore everywhere, even to the grocery store. The closet always smelled like mothballs and wet wool. \n\nBut no, my thoughts aren't disjointed. They make perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that you know are not really there?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes, yeah. Like, if I'm in the stockroom at work, or driving back from a vendor meeting, I'll hear this man's voice. It sounds like he's right behind me, just saying, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he'll start laughing at the inventory picks I just made. And I'll turn around, and there's nobody there. It's really weird. Speaking of driving, though, my car has been making this awful rattling noise lately. It sounds like a bunch of loose change shaking around in a tin can. I think it might be the muffler, but I'm not a car guy. My dad was a car guy, though. He used to spend every weekend in the garage, covered in grease, working on this old Chevy Nova. He loved that car more than anything. He'd always listen to classic rock on this beat-up boombox while he worked. My mom used to complain about the noise all the time, especially when she was trying to watch her soap operas. She never missed an episode of General Hospital. \n\nBut yeah, I do hear that voice sometimes when nobody is around.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear when you're alone is trying to tell you something specific?\n\nPatient: I don't know if he's trying to tell me something specific, really. He just says things like, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he just laughs at me. Like he's judging the inventory picks I just made in the stockroom. It's always right behind me, too, like he's standing right over my shoulder. It happens when I'm driving back from vendor meetings, too. I hate those meetings, honestly. He always smelled like old coffee and peppermint breath mints. He was a nice guy, though. He let me retake a test once when I completely bombed it because I stayed up all night playing video games. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, I don't really think it affects how I talk when it's important. Like I said, it mostly just happens when I'm alone, so I'm not really trying to have a serious conversation with anyone anyway. It's just me and the voice. And it's not like he's trying to have a conversation with me, he's just... commenting.

My grandma's basement used to smell exactly like that. She had this old, green shag carpet down there that was probably from the seventies. We used to play board games on it. Monopoly was her favorite, but she always cheated. She would hide hundred-dollar bills under the board when nobody was looking. I caught her once, and she just laughed and gave me a butterscotch candy. I hate butterscotch. It tastes like old people. 

But no, I don't think the voice messes up my thoughts when I'm actually talking to someone about something important.
