I'm just having normal conversations.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is affecting how your thoughts are organized when you're talking about something important?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't know if it messes up my thoughts when I'm talking to people, because I mostly just hear it when I'm by myself. Like, sometimes when I'm in the stockroom or driving back from a vendor meeting, I'll hear this man's voice. It sounds like it's coming from right behind me, just saying, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he's laughing at my picks. And I'll turn around, and nobody's there. \n\nIt really freaked me out the first time it happened in my car, because my car is tiny. It's this little hatchback I got a few years ago. The air conditioning in it is totally busted, so I have to drive with the windows down all summer. Driving with the windows down on the highway is the worst, though, because the wind just whips your hair right into your eyes. I need to get a haircut, actually. My barber, Tony, he's been cutting my hair since I was like ten. He always gives me a lollipop when I leave, even though I'm twenty-two. It's always the cherry ones. Cherry is okay, but blue raspberry is definitely the best flavor for candy. Jolly Ranchers, especially. I could eat a whole bag of blue raspberry Jolly Ranchers until my tongue turns completely blue. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if the voice makes my thoughts disorganized or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear affects how your thoughts are put together when you're talking about something important?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really think it affects how I talk when it's important. Like I said, it mostly just happens when I'm alone, so I'm not really trying to have a serious conversation with anyone anyway. It's just me and the voice. And it's not like he's trying to have a conversation with me, he's just... commenting. \n\nLike, if I'm doing inventory, he'll just say something about how slow I am. Inventory is the absolute worst part of my job, by the way. You have to count every single box of these tiny little brass fittings, and they all look exactly the same. They come in these huge cardboard boxes that always smell like damp basement. My grandma's basement used to smell exactly like that. She had this old, green shag carpet down there that was probably from the seventies. We used to play board games on it. Monopoly was her favorite, but she always cheated. She would hide hundred-dollar bills under the board when nobody was looking. I caught her once, and she just laughed and gave me a butterscotch candy. I hate butterscotch. It tastes like old people. \n\nBut no, I don't think the voice messes up my thoughts when I'm actually talking to someone about something important.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disjointed or hard to follow when you're talking to friends or family about things that have nothing to do with work?\n\nPatient: I don't think they're hard to follow. My friends and family haven't said anything about me being disjointed or whatever. I was just talking to my mom on the phone the other day, actually, and it was a totally normal conversation. We were mostly just talking about her dog, Buster. He's this huge Golden Retriever mix, and he keeps digging these massive holes all over her backyard. She was telling me how she tried putting cayenne pepper in the dirt to stop him, but he just sneezed a bunch and kept digging anyway. \n\nSpeaking of pepper, I went to this new Thai place with my buddy Dave on Tuesday, and I asked for a level five spicy on my Pad Thai. Big mistake. My mouth was literally on fire for like an hour. We had to go get ice cream right after just to cool it down. I got mint chocolate chip. Mint chocolate chip is the best, but only if the ice cream is actually green, you know? If it's white, it just doesn't taste right for some reason. It's like a mental block or something. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't noticed any issues with my thoughts when I'm just talking to people about regular stuff. Everything seems fine to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are hard to follow when talking to others in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I feel like we kind of just went over this, but no, not really. I talk to people all day and it's totally fine. Like, I was just talking to the cashier at the grocery store last night, and we had a completely normal conversation. \n\nI was there buying cereal because I completely ran out of Cinnamon Toast Crunch. Cinnamon is actually kind of a crazy concept if you really think about it. It's literally just tree bark. Like, who was the first guy in history to look at a tree and say, \"Hey, I'm gonna scrape the bark off that and put it on my oatmeal\"? It had to be someone who was just starving, right? Or maybe they were chopping wood and some sawdust fell in their food. I tried building a birdhouse out of wood once in shop class back in high school. Mr. Henderson was the teacher. He was missing half of his left index finger from a table saw accident way back in the day. He used to point at us with the little stub when we were goofing off in the back of the room. It was hilarious but also kind of gross, honestly. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, no, my thoughts are fine. People understand me just fine in my day-to-day life."
